Theorem 4.1: (i) π_* is the restriction functor i^* (exact). (ii) Rπ_* = π_*. (iii) The Question is equivalent to (★): for all x ∈ P and all F: F(b_x) → (π^{-1}F)(b_x) is an isomorphism. (iv) moreover i^*i_! ≅ Id so π^{-1}F = (i_!F)^~ and the map in (★) is F(b_x) = (i_!F)(b_x) → ((i_!F)^~)(b_x).

Theorem 5.1 (Retraction criterion): Let b ∈ B_P and suppose there exists an infinitesimal homomorphism v₀: b → V₀ to a valuative monoid. Then: (a) every birhizomic family with source b contains a member f_i: b → Q_i admitting a retraction; (b) every birhizomic covering sieve of b is the maximal sieve; (c) for every presheaf F of abelian groups on B_P^op, the canonical map F(b) → (π^{-1}F)(b) is an isomorphism. In particular the conclusion of the Question holds at b.

Proof: (a): cover condition for v₀: ∃i: v₀ = g_i f_i with f_i birhizomic at g_i; apply birhizomicity to factorization through id_b and v₀ (infinitesimal ✓): unique u: Q_i → b with v₀u = g_i ∧ uf_i = id ✓. (b): sieve contains f_i, closed under precomposition with g_i: Q_i → b: hmm: sieve R on b in C = A_P^op: contains the C-arrow (Q_i → b) [the A_P-map f_i: b → Q_i]; g_i: Q_i → b in A_P = C-arrow b → Q_i: sieve closure: for C-arrows: σ: d → b ∈ R and τ: e → d: στ ∈ R: take σ = (Q_i →b) [from f_i] and τ = (b → Q_i) [from g_i]: στ = (b →b) = id_b ∈ R ⟹ R maximal ✓ (sieve containing id_b contains all arrows into b: for any C-arrow δ: c → b: δ = id_b ∘ δ ∈ R ✓). ✓. (c): with all covering sieves of b maximal: sheafification fixes values at b: L := i_!F: L^+(b) = colim_{R ∈ Cov(b)} Match(R, L) = Match(max, L) ≅ L(b); iterate ✓ L~(b) = L(b) = F(b) ✓ (with (i^*i_! ≅ id) ✓). Now, also mention the interpretation: the Question is the birhizomic analogue of topological invariance of étale cohomology [SGA4, VIII] — where the analogous statement holds because étale maps lift uniquely through infinitesimal thickenings; here the role of "infinitesimal thickenings" is played by infinitesimal monoid maps, and the statement at valuative points holds by the same mechanism ✓ nice scholarly framing.

Alright — also the honest bottom line: "We prove the Question in the valuative-point case; the general case is reduced to the following precise open problem: (Open Problem) for every fine sharp saturated monoid b of rank ≥ 2 (arising as P[-x]^♯), does every birhizomic family with source b contain a member admitting a retraction — or, more generally, is sheafification of i_!F trivial at b for all F? We show the infinitesimal-thickening method cannot settle it (Theorem A)."

Abstract: "We study the comparison morphism between the birhizomic topology on the category of monoids under P and the trivial topology on the category of localizations... We prove that the right adjoint π_* is simply the (exact) restriction functor, so the question reduces to a pointwise statement about sheafification. We prove the pointwise statement at every point admitting an infinitesimal map to a valuative monoid — in particular at every valuative point — via a retraction criterion, giving a full affirmative answer for monoids of rank ≤ 1. We then prove that no infinitesimal maps from fine monoids of rank ≥ 2 to valuative monoids exist, showing that the retraction method is exhausted and the general case is reduced to a precise open problem, which we formulate."
